| **`RXNO:0000127`** | **Balz-Schiemann reaction** | **`is_a`:** aromatic substitution step (RXNO:0000332)<br>**Synonyme:** Schiemann reaction | The conversion of an aromatic amine to the corresponding fluoride via the diazonium fluoroborate. |
| **`RXNO:0000128`** | **Kolbe electrolysis** | **`is_a`:** planned reaction step (RXNO:0000001), carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** Crum-Brown-Walker reaction, Kolbe electrolytic synthesis, Kolbe electrosynthesis, Kolbe reaction | The decarboxylation and coupling of a carboxylate or mixture of carboxylates to give a dimer. |
| **`RXNO:0000129`** | **Ugi reaction** | **`is_a`:** dehydration reaction (MOP:0000628), molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** 4CC, Ugi four-component condensation, Ugi-4CR | Reaction of a ketone or aldehyde, amine, an isocyanide and carboxylic acid to form a bisamide. |
| **`RXNO:0000130`** | **Wacker-Tsuji oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** Wacker oxidation, Wacker reaction | The oxidation of a terminal alkene or ethene to a methyl ketone or acetaldehyde, catalysed by PdCl2 and CuCl2, and using molecular oxygen as the oxidant. |
| **`RXNO:0000131`** | **Wacker process** | **`is_a`:** Wacker-Tsuji oxidation (RXNO:0000130) | The oxidation of ethene to acetaldehyde, catalysed by PdCl2 and CuCl2, and using molecular oxygen as the oxidant. |
| **`RXNO:0000132`** | **Monsanto process** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | The reaction of carbon monoxide with methanol to form acetic acid, catalysed by a rhodium complex. |
| **`RXNO:0000133`** | **Cativa process** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | The reaction of carbon monoxide with methanol to form acetic acid, catalysed by an iridium complex. |
| **`RXNO:0000134`** | **Barton-McCombie deoxygenation** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037)<br>**Synonyme:** Barton deoxygenation, Barton reduction | The deoxygenation of an alcohol to the corresponding alkane using a thiochloroformate. It proceeds by a radical mechanism. |
| **`RXNO:0000135`** | **Barton decarboxylation** | **`is_a`:** end-of-chain chain shortening (RXNO:0000105) | The decarboxylation of a carboxylic acid to the lower homologue alkane using a pyridine thione. |
| **`RXNO:0000136`** | **Rosenmund reduction** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037)<br>**Synonyme:** Rosenmund reaction | The hydrogenolysis of an acyl halide to the corresponding aldehyde, catalysed by palladium-on-barium sulfate. |
| **`RXNO:0000137`** | **Sonogashira coupling** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000), palladium-catalysed carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000316)<br>**Synonyme:** Hagihara-Sonogashira coupling, Sonogashira cross-coupling, Sonogashira reaction | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction where a terminal alkyne reacts with an aryl halide to give an aryl alkyne. It is catalysed by palladium. |
| **`RXNO:0000138`** | **benzoin condensation** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** benzoin addition | A reaction between two aromatic aldehydes to give a benzoin (an alpha-hydroxyketone). |
| **`RXNO:0000139`** | **Gattermann reaction** | **`is_a`:** formylation (MOP:0000003), planned reaction step (RXNO:0000001), carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | The formylation of an arene with hydrogen cyanide to give an aryl aldehyde or ketone. |
| **`RXNO:0000140`** | **Suzuki-Miyaura coupling** | **`is_a`:** Suzuki-type coupling (RXNO:0000592)<br>**Synonyme:** Suzuki coupling reaction, Suzuki cross-coupling, Suzuki cross-coupling reaction, Suzuki reaction, Suzuki-Miyaura coupling, Suzuki-Miyaura coupling reaction, Suzuki-Miyaura cross-coupling, Suzuki-Miyaura cross-coupling reaction, Suzuki-Miyaura reaction | The coupling of a boronic acid or boronic ester with an unsaturated halide, triflate or sulfonate catalysed by a palladium(0) complex. |
| **`RXNO:0000141`** | **Sharpless asymmetric epoxidation** | **`is_a`:** epoxidation (MOP:0000671), oxacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000346)<br>**Synonyme:** Katsuki-Sharpless asymmetric epoxidation, Katsuki-Sharpless epoxidation, Sharpless epoxidation, Sharpless-Katsuki asymmetric epoxidation, Sharpless-Katsuki epoxidation | The epoxidation of an allylic alcohol to give a beta,gamma-epoxy alcohol, with control of absolute stereochemistry at the two new stereocentres. The oxidising agent is tert-butyl hydroperoxide, and the catalyst is a complex of titanium salt and optically active diethyl tartrate. |
| **`RXNO:0000142`** | **Sharpless asymmetric dihydroxylation** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041)<br>**Synonyme:** Sharpless bishydroxylation, Sharpless hydroxylation | The dihydroxylation of an alkene to give an alpha,beta-diol, with control of absolute stereochemistry at the two new stereocentres. The oxidising agent is osmium tetroxide, which is used catalytically with a stoichiometric oxidant and a chiral ligand. The reagents are available commercially as AD-mix. |
| **`RXNO:0000143`** | **Sharpless asymmetric aminohydroxylation** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041)<br>**Synonyme:** Sharpless oxyamination | The aminohydroxylation of an alkene to give a beta-amino alcohol, with control of absolute stereochemistry at the two new stereocentres. The oxidising agent is an oxygen-containing osmium species and/or an amino derivative which can be used catalytically with a stoichiometric oxidant and a chiral ligand. |
| **`RXNO:0000144`** | **Kumada coupling** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** Kharasch cross-coupling, Kumada cross-coupling | A cross-coupling reaction between a Grignard reagent and an activated (aryl or vinyl) halide or triflate. The reaction is catalysed by a palladium or nickel catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000145`** | **Barbier-Wieland degradation** | **`is_a`:** end-of-chain chain shortening (RXNO:0000105)<br>**Synonyme:** Barbier-Wieland procedure | The degradation of a carboxylic acid to its next lower homologous carboxylic acid. |
| **`RXNO:0000146`** | **Corey-Fuchs reaction** | **`is_a`:** chain lengthening (RXNO:0000070)<br>**Synonyme:** Ramirez-Corey-Fuchs reaction | A homologation reaction of an aldehyde to yield a terminal alkyne. The reagents are tetrabromomethane, triphenylphosphine, butyllithium and water. |
| **`RXNO:0000147`** | **Emde degradation** | **`is_a`:** cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073)<br>**Synonyme:** Emde reaction, Emde reduction | A reduction of a quaternary ammonium salt containing at least one unsaturated substituent, to give a tertiary amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000148`** | **Claisen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement (MOP:0000721), rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement of an allyl vinyl ether to give a gamma,delta-unsaturated carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000149`** | **Ireland-Claisen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement (MOP:0000721), rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The enolisation and subsequent [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement of an allylic acetate to give a gamma,delta-unsaturated carboxylic acid. |
| **`RXNO:0000150`** | **joining with rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | A reaction in which one or more atoms are added to the molecular skeleton and a skeletal rearrangement occurs. |
| **`RXNO:0000151`** | **Bellus-Claisen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement (MOP:0000721), joining with rearrangement (RXNO:0000150) | The coupling and subsequent [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement of an allylic ether or allylic thioether or allylic amine and a ketene to give the corresponding gamma,delta-unsaturated carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000152`** | **Eschenmoser-Claisen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement (MOP:0000721), joining with rearrangement (RXNO:0000150) | The coupling and subsequent [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement of an allylic alcohol and alpha,alpha-dialkoxy ethylamine to give a gamma,delta-unsaturated dialkyl amide. |
| **`RXNO:0000153`** | **Johnson-Claisen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement (MOP:0000721), joining with rearrangement (RXNO:0000150) | The coupling and subsequent [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement of an allylic alcohol and trimethyl orthoacetate to give a gamma,delta-unsaturated alkyl ester. |
| **`RXNO:0000154`** | **Swern oxidation** | **`is_a`:** alcohol oxidation (MOP:0000572), functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012) | The oxidation of a primary alcohol or secondary alcohol to an aldehyde or ketone respectively. Does not work with allylic or benzylic alcohols. |
| **`RXNO:0000155`** | **Finkelstein reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The conversion of an alkyl chloride, alkyl bromide or alkyl sulfonate ester to an alkyl iodide by SN2 substitution. The reaction relies upon the equilibrium being pushed to completion by the precipitation. |
| **`RXNO:0000156`** | **Lossen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The derivatisation (for example with tosyl chloride) and subsequent rearrangement of a hydroxamic acid to give an isocyanate. |
| **`RXNO:0000157`** | **Nef reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The conversion of a primary nitroalkane or secondary nitroalkane to an aldehyde or ketone, respectively. |
| **`RXNO:0000158`** | **Japp-Klingemann reaction** | **`is_a`:** cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073) | The reaction of a doubly activated methylene compound that has at least one carbonyl substituent with an aryl azide to give a hydrazone. Usually there is concomitant cleaving and oxidation of one of the carbonyl substituents. |
| **`RXNO:0000159`** | **Doebner-Miller reaction** | **`is_a`:** fused-ring-system formation (RXNO:0000052)<br>**Synonyme:** Doebner-von Miller reaction, Skraup-Doebner-von Miller quinoline synthesis | The formation of a quinoline from a primary aromatic amine and an alpha,beta-unsaturated carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000160`** | **Fmoc protection** | **`is_a`:** amino group protection (RXNO:0000321) | The protection of an amine (often a peptide) as its 9H-fluoren-9-ylmethoxycarbonyl derivative. |
| **`RXNO:0000161`** | **Paal-Knorr synthesis** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | The cyclisation of a 1,4-dicarbonyl compound with another molecule to give a five-membered aromatic ring compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000162`** | **Paal-Knorr furan synthesis** | **`is_a`:** Paal-Knorr synthesis (RXNO:0000161), oxacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000346) | The cyclisation of a 1,4-dicarbonyl compound with an acid catalyst to give a furan. |
| **`RXNO:0000163`** | **Paal-Knorr thiophene synthesis** | **`is_a`:** Paal-Knorr synthesis (RXNO:0000161), thiacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000348) | The cyclisation of a 1,4-dicarbonyl compound with phosphorus pentasulfide to give a thiophene. |
| **`RXNO:0000164`** | **Paal-Knorr pyrrole synthesis** | **`is_a`:** Paal-Knorr synthesis (RXNO:0000161), pyrrole synthesis (RXNO:0000497) | The cyclisation of a 1,4-dicarbonyl compound with an amine to give a pyrrole. |
| **`RXNO:0000165`** | **Schotten-Baumann reaction** | **`is_a`:** substitution step (RXNO:0000331) | The coupling of an alcohol or amine with an acyl chloride to give an ester or amide. A two-phase solvent system is often used, to enable the acid generated during the reaction to be neutralised by the aqueous base, while the starting materials and products remain in the organic phase. |
| **`RXNO:0000166`** | **Hofmann elimination** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton elimination reaction (RXNO:0000123)<br>**Synonyme:** exhaustive methylation, Hofmann degradation | The elimination of a methylated amine from an alkylamine to form an olefin. The reaction proceeds via the quaternary ammonium salt. |
| **`RXNO:0000167`** | **Fischer-Speier esterification** | **`is_a`:** substitution step (RXNO:0000331)<br>**Synonyme:** Fischer esterification | The esterification of a carboxylic acid with an alcohol. Various Lewis acids or Bronsted acids may be used as catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000168`** | **Weerman reaction** | **`is_a`:** end-of-chain chain shortening (RXNO:0000105)<br>**Synonyme:** Weerman degradation, Weerman's reaction | The degradation of an alpha-hydroxy amide or an alpha,beta-unsaturated amide to the lower homologue aldehyde, especially in carbohydrate chemistry. The process proceeds via Hofmann degradation. |
| **`RXNO:0000169`** | **Dakin reaction** | **`is_a`:** cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073) | The reaction of ortho- or para-phenolic (or ortho- or para-anilinic) aldehydes or ketones with hydrogen peroxide to form bisphenols (or hydroxy anilines) and carboxylic acids. |
| **`RXNO:0000170`** | **Schmidt rearrangement, carboxylic acid** | **`is_a`:** end-of-chain chain shortening (RXNO:0000105) | The reaction of a carboxylic acid with hydrazoic acid to give the lower homologue amine, with the loss of the terminal carbon atom. |
| **`RXNO:0000171`** | **Schmidt rearrangement, ketone** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton insertion reaction (RXNO:0000030) | The reaction of a ketone with hydrazoic acid with the insertion of an NH group between the carbonyl carbon and one of the R groups to form an amide. |
| **`RXNO:0000172`** | **Schmidt rearrangement, tertiary alcohol** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton insertion reaction (RXNO:0000030) | The reaction of a tertiary alcohol with hydrazoic acid with rearrangement to form an imide. |
| **`RXNO:0000173`** | **Schmidt rearrangement, alkene** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton insertion reaction (RXNO:0000030) | The reaction of an alkene with hydrazoic acid with rearrangement to form an imide. |
| **`RXNO:0000174`** | **Sommelet-Hauser rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The [2,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement of a benzyl quaternary ammonium salt to form an aniline. |
| **`RXNO:0000175`** | **Dakin-West reaction** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | The reaction of an amino acid with an acid anhydride to give an alpha-acylamido ketone. The reaction proceeds via the mixed anhydride and an oxazolone; the product is always racemic. |
| **`RXNO:0000176`** | **Beckmann fragmentation** | **`is_a`:** cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073) | The cleavage of an oxime to the corresponding nitrile and an alkyl halide. This reaction is related to the Beckmann rearrangement, and occurs when the R group that is anti to the oxime hydroxyl is quaternary. |
| **`RXNO:0000177`** | **Kharasch reaction** | **`is_a`:** chain lengthening (RXNO:0000070)<br>**Synonyme:** Kharasch addition reaction | The transfer of a hydrogen or halogen atom from a haloalkane to an alkene, giving the chain-extended halogenated alkane. |
| **`RXNO:0000178`** | **Kharasch-Sosnovsky reaction** | **`is_a`:** substitution step (RXNO:0000331) | The allylic oxidation of an alkene with a peroxy ester or a peroxide to give the corresponding allylic alcohol derivative. The catalyst is a copper compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000179`** | **Elbs persulfate oxidation, phenol variant** | **`is_a`:** aromatic substitution step (RXNO:0000332) | The oxidation of a phenol to give a (predominantly para) diphenol using potassium persulfate. |
| **`RXNO:0000180`** | **Elbs persulfate oxidation, tolyl variant** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005), functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012) | The oxidation of a methyl-substituted aromatic compound to the corresponding benzaldehyde using potassium persulfate. |
| **`RXNO:0000181`** | **Boyland-Sims oxidation** | **`is_a`:** aromatic substitution step (RXNO:0000332) | The oxidation of an aromatic amine to give a (predominantly ortho) amino aryl sulfate using potassium persulfate followed by hydrolysis to yield the corresponding aminophenol. |
| **`RXNO:0000182`** | **Kolbe-Schmitt reaction** | **`is_a`:** carboxylation (MOP:0000591), carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | The (predominantly ortho) carboxylation of a phenoxide. |
| **`RXNO:0000183`** | **Perkow reaction** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | The reaction between an alpha-halocarbonyl compound and a trialkyl phosphite to give an enol phosphate. |
| **`RXNO:0000184`** | **Pfitzner-Moffatt oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** Moffatt oxidation | The oxidation of a primary or secondary alcohol to the corresponding aldehyde or ketone respectively using dimethyl sulfoxide, 1,3-dicyclohexylcarbodiimide and acid. | **`RXNO:0000185`** | **Willgerodt reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The conversion of an aryl alkyl ketone to an amide and/or an ammonium salt of a carboxylic acid using ammonium polysulfide. The carbonyl group of the product is always at the end of the alkyl chain. |
| **`RXNO:0000186`** | **Willgerodt-Kindler reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The conversion of an aryl alkyl ketone to an amide using sulfur and dry ammonia or a primary or secondary amine. The amide carbonyl group is always at the end of the alkyl chain. |
| **`RXNO:0000187`** | **Tishchenko reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The formation of an ester from two aldehydes via a disproportionation process using a metal alkoxide to oxidise one aldehyde and reduce the other. |
| **`RXNO:0000188`** | **Zimmermann reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | The reaction between a methylene ketone and a polynitro aromatic compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000189`** | **Neber rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The rearrangement of the sulfonic ester of a ketoxime in base to give an alpha-amino ketone. |
| **`RXNO:0000191`** | **Nozaki-Hiyama-Kishi reaction** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** Nozaki reaction, Nozaki-Hiyama-Kishi coupling, Nozaki-Kishi reaction | A coupling reaction of an aldehyde to an allyl, vinyl or aryl halide or triflate in the presence of chromium(II). | **`RXNO:0000192`** | **Buchwald-Hartwig amination** | **`is_a`:** aromatic substitution step (RXNO:0000332)<br>**Synonyme:** Buchwald-Hartwig reaction | A reaction in which an aryl halide or aryl triflate reacts with a primary or secondary amine in the presence of base to give a secondary or tertiary amine. It is catalysed by a palladium catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000193`** | **Hiyama coupling** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** Hiyama cross-coupling | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction where an organosilane reacts with an organic halide or triflate. It is catalysed by a palladium or nickel complex. |
| **`RXNO:0000194`** | **Stevens rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The 1,2-rearrangement of an alpha-methylene ammonium salt or alpha-methylene sulfonium salt to an amine or sulfide in the presence of strong base. |
| **`RXNO:0000195`** | **Stevens rearrangement, ammonium** | **`is_a`:** Stevens rearrangement (RXNO:0000194) | The 1,2-rearrangement of an alpha-methylene ammonium salt to an amine in the presence of strong base. |
| **`RXNO:0000196`** | **Stevens rearrangement, sulfonium** | **`is_a`:** Stevens rearrangement (RXNO:0000194) | The 1,2-rearrangement of an alpha-methylene sulfonium salt to a sulfide in the presence of strong base. |
| **`RXNO:0000197`** | **aza-Cope rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement (MOP:0000721), rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | A [3,3]-sigmatropic rearrangement where an aza-1,5-hexadiene rearranges to form an aza-1,5-hexadiene. |
| **`RXNO:0000198`** | **2-aza-Cope rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** aza-Cope rearrangement (RXNO:0000197) | The rearrangement of a 2-aza-1,5-hexadiene to form an imine. |
| **`RXNO:0000199`** | **3-aza-Cope rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** aza-Cope rearrangement (RXNO:0000197)<br>**Synonyme:** amino-Claisen rearrangement, aza-Claisen rearrangement | The rearrangement of a 3-aza-1,5-hexadiene to form a delta,epsilon-unsaturated imine. |
| **`RXNO:0000200`** | **1-aza-Cope rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** aza-Cope rearrangement (RXNO:0000197) | The rearrangement of a delta,epsilon-unsaturated imine to form a 3-aza-1,5-hexadiene. |
| **`RXNO:0000201`** | **Steglich rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The rearrangement of an oxazolyl carbonate to a C-carboxyazlactone. |
| **`RXNO:0000202`** | **ring rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | A reaction in which the number of rings in a molecule stays the same but the connectivity of the atoms in at least one ring changes. |
| **`RXNO:0000203`** | **deprotection reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional modification (RXNO:0000010) | A reaction in which a protecting group is modified by converting it into a functional group. |
| **`RXNO:0000204`** | **multiple-bond metathesis** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** metathesis, multiple bond metathesis | A joining reaction in which two carbon-carbon multiple bonds are redistributed among two fragments. | **`RXNO:0000205`** | **ring breaking** | **`is_a`:** planned reaction step (RXNO:0000001)<br>**Synonyme:** ring opening, selective ring opening | A planned reaction step in which the product has fewer rings than the reactants. |
| **`RXNO:0000206`** | **ring contraction** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | A planned reaction step in which an existing ring is reduced in size. |
| **`RXNO:0000207`** | **Strecker reaction** | **`is_a`:** chain lengthening (RXNO:0000070) | An addition reaction between a carbonyl compound, an inorganic cyanide and an ammonium salt, followed by hydrolysis, to give an alpha-amino carboxylic acid. |

| **`RXNO:0000209`** | **Nazarov cyclisation** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** Nazarov cyclization | The acid-catalysed cyclisation of a divinylketone to give a cyclopentenone. |

| **`RXNO:0000211`** | **Tamao oxidation** | **`is_a`:** substitution step (RXNO:0000331)<br>**Synonyme:** Tamao-Kumada oxidation | The stereospecific oxidation of a (halosilyl)alkane to the corresponding alcohol using potassium fluoride and hydrogen peroxide. |
| **`RXNO:0000212`** | **Sakurai reaction, aldehyde or ketone** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000), Sakurai reaction (RXNO:0000443) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction in which an aldehyde or ketone reacts with allyltrimethylsilane to form a homoallylic alcohol. |
| **`RXNO:0000213`** | **Sakurai reaction, enone** | **`is_a`:** Sakurai reaction (RXNO:0000443) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction in which an enone reacts with allyltrimethylsilane to form a delta,epsilon-unsaturated carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000214`** | **Sakurai reaction, acid chloride** | **`is_a`:** Sakurai reaction (RXNO:0000443) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction in which an acid chloride reacts with allyltrimethylsilane to form a gamma,delta-unsaturated ketone. |
| **`RXNO:0000215`** | **Sakurai reaction, acetal or ketal** | **`is_a`:** Sakurai reaction (RXNO:0000443) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction in which an acetal or ketal reacts with allyltrimethylsilane to form a homoallylic ether. |
| **`RXNO:0000216`** | **imine Sakurai reaction** | **`is_a`:** Sakurai reaction (RXNO:0000443) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction in which an imine reacts with allyltrimethylsilane to form a homoallylic amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000217`** | **Sakurai reaction, epoxide** | **`is_a`:** ring breaking (RXNO:0000205), Sakurai reaction (RXNO:0000443) | A ring-breaking reaction in which an epoxide reacts with allyltrimethylsilane to form a hydroxy alkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000218`** | **Cannizzaro reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The disproportionation of a non-enolisable aldehyde to the corresponding carboxylic acid and alcohol. The reaction is base-catalysed. |
| **`RXNO:0000219`** | **crossed Cannizzaro reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The reaction between two different non-enolisable aldehydes to give a carboxylic acid and an alcohol. The reaction is base-catalysed. |
| **`RXNO:0000220`** | **Pummerer rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011)<br>**Synonyme:** Pummerer methyl sulfoxide rearrangement | The conversion of a sulfoxide with an alpha-hydrogen to an alpha-substituted thioether. | **`RXNO:0000221`** | **Polonovski reaction** | **`is_a`:** cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073) | The reaction of a tertiary amine oxide with an anhydride or acyl chloride to give an N,N-disubstituted acetamide and an aldehyde. |
| **`RXNO:0000222`** | **Polonovski-Potier reaction** | **`is_a`:** Polonovski reaction (RXNO:0000221)<br>**Synonyme:** Potier-Polonovski reaction | The reaction of a tertiary amine oxide with trifluoroacetic anhydride to give an N,N-disubstituted acetamide and an aldehyde. |
| **`RXNO:0000223`** | **Menshutkin reaction** | **`is_a`:** N-alkylation (MOP:0002369), functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** Menschutkin reaction | The alkylation of a tertiary amine with an alkyl halide to the corresponding ammonium salt. |
| **`RXNO:0000224`** | **Strecker degradation** | **`is_a`:** end-of-chain chain shortening (RXNO:0000105) | The deamination and decarboxylation of amino acids to imines and then to aldehydes or ketones, in the presence of a carbonyl compound (especially an alpha,beta-dicarbonyl compound or a reducing sugar such as glucose). | **`RXNO:0000225`** | **Wohl-Ziegler bromination** | **`is_a`:** functional group addition step (RXNO:0000373)<br>**Synonyme:** Wohl-Ziegler reaction | The allylic or benzylic bromination of hydrocarbons using an N-bromoimide and a radical initiator, to give an allylic or benzylic bromide. |
| **`RXNO:0000226`** | **Wolff-Kishner reduction** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037) | The reduction of an aldehyde or ketone to the corresponding methylene compound, using hydrazine hydrate and a base. |
| **`RXNO:0000227`** | **Wolff-Kishner reduction, Huang-Minlon modification** | **`is_a`:** Wolff-Kishner reduction (RXNO:0000226)<br>**Synonyme:** Huang-Minlon modification | The reduction of an aldehyde or ketone to the corresponding methylene compound, using hydrazine hydrate and a base in refluxing ethylene glycol. |
| **`RXNO:0000228`** | **Zemplen transesterification** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037)<br>**Synonyme:** Zemplen procedure | The reduction of an ester to the corresponding alcohol, using sodium methoxide in dry methanol. |
| **`RXNO:0000229`** | **Ferrier rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025)<br>**Synonyme:** Ferrier reaction | The rearrangement of an O-alkenyl acetal to give the corresponding beta-alkoxy carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000230`** | **aza-Ferrier rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025)<br>**Synonyme:** aza-Ferrier reaction | The rearrangement of an O-alkenyl hemiaminal ether to give the corresponding beta-amino carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000231`** | **aza-ene reaction** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | A joining reaction where an N=C system with an allylic hydrogen reacts with a compound containing a multiple bond (an enophile) to form a substituted alkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000232`** | **Petasis reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011)<br>**Synonyme:** Petasis three-component coupling | The alkylation of an amine with a boronic acid and an aldehyde to give a substituted amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000233`** | **Achmatowicz rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** ring rearrangement (RXNO:0000202) | A rearrangement of a furfuryl alcohol derivative to a dihydropyranone, from which monosaccharides may be synthesised. |
| **`RXNO:0000234`** | **aza-Achmatowicz rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** ring rearrangement (RXNO:0000202) | A rearrangement of a furfuryl amine derivative to a tetrahydropyridinone. |
| **`RXNO:0000235`** | **aza-Henry reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction between a nitroalkane and an imine to give a beta-amino nitroalkane. |
| **`RXNO:0000236`** | **Biginelli reaction** | **`is_a`:** dehydration reaction (MOP:0000628), ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** Biginelli condensation, Biginelli pyrimidone synthesis, Biginelli three-component reaction | A three-component coupling of a beta-keto ester, an aldehyde and urea to give a 3,4-dihydropyrimidinone. |
| **`RXNO:0000237`** | **Blaise reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | The coupling of an alpha-bromo ester with a nitrile to give a beta-ketoester, in the presence of zinc. |
| **`RXNO:0000238`** | **Katritzky pyrylium-pyridinium method** | **`is_a`:** substitution step (RXNO:0000331) | The substitution of an amine with a nucleophile using a pyrylium salt. |
| **`RXNO:0000239`** | **metalla-aza-Claisen rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The rearrangement of a 3-aza-4-metalla-1,5-hexadiene to form a delta,epsilon-unsaturated metallaimine. |
| **`RXNO:0000240`** | **Bergman cyclisation** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** Bergman cyclization, Bergman reaction, Masamune-Bergman cyclisation, Masamune-Bergman cyclization | The cyclisation of an ene-diyne to give a 1,4-diradical cyclohexatriene. The diradical usually aromatises. |
| **`RXNO:0000241`** | **nitro-Michael reaction** | **`is_a`:** Michael addition (RXNO:0000009) | A conjugate addition reaction where a carbon nucleophile adds to an alpha,beta-unsaturated nitro compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000242`** | **Noyori asymmetric hydrogenation, alkene** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041)<br>**Synonyme:** Noyori hydrogenation, alkene | The homogeneous asymmetric catalytic hydrogenation of a C=C double bond using a ruthenium(II) BINAP catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000243`** | **Noyori asymmetric hydrogenation, carbonyl** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037)<br>**Synonyme:** Noyori hydrogenation, carbonyl | The homogeneous asymmetric catalytic hydrogenation of a carbonyl bond using a ruthenium(II) BINAP catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000244`** | **Passerini reaction** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | The reaction between an isonitrile, a carboxylic acid and aldehyde or ketone to give an alpha-acyloxy amide. |
| **`RXNO:0000245`** | **ring-closing metathesis** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** RCM, ring closing alkene metathesis, ring closing metathesis, ring closing olefin metathesis, ring-closing alkene metathesis, ring-closing olefin metathesis | A joining reaction in which two carbon-carbon double bonds in the same molecule are redistributed to give a cycloalkene. It is typically catalysed by a metal carbenoid, and proceeds via two four-membered intermediates (metallacyclobutanes). The reaction is driven by release of a low-MW alkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000246`** | **polymerisation reaction** | **`is_a`:** polymerisation (MOP:0000629), molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** polymerisation, polymerization, polymerization reaction | A joining reaction, the objective of which is that a large number of monomers react together to produce a polymer consisting of repeating units. |
| **`RXNO:0000247`** | **ring-opening metathesis polymerisation** | **`is_a`:** polymerisation reaction (RXNO:0000246)<br>**Synonyme:** ROMP, ring-opening metathesis polymerization | A joining reaction in which two carbon-carbon double bonds are redistributed among two fragments, i.e. two alkenes. It is typically catalysed by a metal carbenoid, and proceeds via two four-membered intermediates (metallacyclobutanes). The reaction is driven by release of ring strain in the cycloalkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000248`** | **Carroll rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025)<br>**Synonyme:** Kimel-Cope rearrangement | An adaptation of the Claisen rearrangement that transforms a beta-keto allyl ester into a gamma,delta-allyl ketone. It uses base and high temperature or milder conditions with a ruthenium or palladium complex as catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000249`** | **Bose-Mitsunobu reaction** | **`is_a`:** Mitsunobu reaction (RXNO:0000034) | A Mitsunobu reaction, where an alcohol is converted to something else with inversion at the stereocentre, using diphenylphosphoryl azide. |
| **`RXNO:0000250`** | **Fritsch-Buttenberg-Wiechell rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The rearrangement of a 2-bromoalkene into an alkyne, mediated by strong base. |
| **`RXNO:0000251`** | **Stork-Wittig olefination** | **`is_a`:** olefination (RXNO:0000479) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction where an aldehyde reacts with a phosphonium salt to give a Z-iodoalkene. The reaction is mediated by sodium or potassium hexamethyldisilazide. |
| **`RXNO:0000252`** | **Cadogan carbazole synthesis** | **`is_a`:** carbazole synthesis (RXNO:0000521) | The reduction of a 2-nitrobiaryl compound to give a carbazole. |
| **`RXNO:0000253`** | **Cadogan coupling** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | The arylation of an arylamine mediated by isopentyl nitrite. |
| **`RXNO:0000254`** | **MacDonald condensation** | **`is_a`:** fused-ring-system formation (RXNO:0000052) | The reaction between a 1,9-diformyldipyrromethane and a dipyrromethane to give a porphodimethene. |
| **`RXNO:0000255`** | **Zweifel diene synthesis** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon homocoupling reaction (RXNO:0000039) | The carbon-carbon homocoupling reaction of a pair of alkyne molecules using a borane and iodine. The reaction proceeds via a borane intermediate. |
| **`RXNO:0000256`** | **Dess-Martin oxidation** | **`is_a`:** alcohol oxidation (MOP:0000572), functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** Dess-Martin periodinane oxidation | The oxidation of a primary alcohol to an aldehyde or a secondary alcohol to a ketone using the Dess-Martin periodinane. |
| **`RXNO:0000257`** | **Criegee oxidation** | **`is_a`:** alkene oxidative cleavage (MOP:0000708), cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073)<br>**Synonyme:** Criegee glycol cleavage, Criegee reaction | The oxidative cleavage of an alpha,beta-diol using lead tetraacetate to give the corresponding carbonyl compounds. |
| **`RXNO:0000258`** | **Simmons-Smith reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** Simmons-Smith cyclopropanation | A ring formation reaction where an alkene reacts with a dihalomethane in the presence of a metal atom or metal complexes to form a cyclopropane. The traditional reaction uses a zinc-copper couple and diiodomethane. |
| **`RXNO:0000259`** | **Wittig rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | Any of a family of sigmatropic rearrangements that involve the migration of a group on an electronegative atom (O or N) to a carbon atom. |
| **`RXNO:0000260`** | **[1,2]-Wittig rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** Wittig rearrangement (RXNO:0000259)<br>**Synonyme:** 1,2-Wittig rearrangement, [1,2] Wittig rearrangement | A base-mediated 1,2-sigmatropic rearrangement of an ether to give an alcohol. |
| **`RXNO:0000261`** | **[2,3]-Wittig rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** Wittig rearrangement (RXNO:0000259)<br>**Synonyme:** 2,3-Wittig rearrangement, [2,3] Wittig rearrangement | A base-mediated 2,3-sigmatropic rearrangement of an allyl ether to give a homoallylic alcohol. |
| **`RXNO:0000262`** | **[1,2]-aza-Wittig rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** Wittig rearrangement (RXNO:0000259)<br>**Synonyme:** 1,2-aza-Wittig rearrangement, [1,2] aza-Wittig rearrangement | A base-mediated 1,2-sigmatropic rearrangement of a tertiary amine to give a secondary amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000263`** | **[2,3]-aza-Wittig rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** Wittig rearrangement (RXNO:0000259)<br>**Synonyme:** 2,3-aza-Wittig rearrangement, [2,3] aza-Wittig rearrangement | A base-mediated 2,3-sigmatropic rearrangement of an activated tertiary allyl amine, to give a secondary homoallylic amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000264`** | **aza-Baylis-Hillman reaction** | **`is_a`:** Baylis-Hillman reaction (RXNO:0000076)<br>**Synonyme:** aza-Morita-Baylis-Hillman reaction | A carbon-carbon coupling of an imine to an activated alkene in the presence of a nucleophilic base to give an allylic amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000265`** | **aza-Wittig reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbonyl compound to imine conversion (RXNO:0000367) | Reaction of a carbonyl compound with a phosphazene to give an imine. |
| **`RXNO:0000266`** | **Fenton reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** Fenton's reaction | An oxidation reaction involving Fenton's reagent. |
| **`RXNO:0000268`** | **Hantzsch pyridine synthesis** | **`is_a`:** pyridine synthesis (RXNO:0000520)<br>**Synonyme:** Hantzsch dihydropyridine synthesis | A ring-forming reaction between two equivalents of a beta-ketoester, an aldehyde and ammonia (or an ammonium salt), to give a dihydropyridine. The dihydropyridine can be oxidised to a pyridine using an oxidising agent. |
| **`RXNO:0000269`** | **alkyne-azide click reaction** | **`is_a`:** [3+2] cycloaddition (MOP:0006371), 1,3-dipolar cycloaddition (RXNO:0000018), azacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000347)<br>**Synonyme:** Huisgen cycloaddition, Huisgen reaction, Huisgen's 1,3-dipolar cycloaddition, "click" reaction, 'click' reaction, alkyne-azide Huisgen cycloaddition, alkyne-azide Huisgen reaction, azide-alkyne Huisgen cycloaddition, azide-alkyne Huisgen reaction, azide-alkyne click reaction, click reaction | A [3+2] cycloaddition reaction between an azide and an alkyne to give a 1,2,3-triazole, needing only heat to proceed. |
| **`RXNO:0000270`** | **copper(I)-catalyzed azide-alkyne cycloaddition** | **`is_a`:** [3+2] cycloaddition (MOP:0006371), azacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000347)<br>**Synonyme:** CuAAC, copper(I)-catalysed alkyne-azide cycloaddition, copper(I)-catalysed azide-alkyne click reaction, copper(I)-catalysed azide-alkyne cycloaddition, copper(I)-catalyzed alkyne-azide cycloaddition, copper(I)-catalyzed azide-alkyne click reaction, copper(I)-catalyzed azide-alkyne cycloaddition | A [3+2] cycloaddition reaction between an azide and an alkyne to give a 1,2,3-triazole as a single regioisomer. |
| **`RXNO:0000271`** | **Fischer-Tropsch process** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** Fischer-Tropsch reaction, Fischer-Tropsch synthesis | A process in which carbon monoxide and hydrogen ('syngas') are converted into various liquid hydrocarbons. |
| **`RXNO:0000272`** | **hydroformylation** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000) | The reaction of hydrogen and carbon monoxide with an alkene, to give an aldehyde, catalysed by a rhodium or cobalt catalyst. The process is equivalent to the formal addition of the H-C bond of formaldehyde across the double bond of an alkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000273`** | **retro-Diels-Alder reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring breaking (RXNO:0000205)<br>**Synonyme:** retro Diels-Alder reaction | The reverse of a [4+2] cycloaddition reaction, in which an unsaturated six-membered ring compound fragments to give a diene and a compound containing a double or triple bond (a dienophile). |
| **`RXNO:0000274`** | **retro-aldol addition** | **`is_a`:** cleaving reaction (RXNO:0000073)<br>**Synonyme:** retro-aldol reaction | The reverse of an aldol addition, in which a beta-hydroxy ketone fragments to form a carbonyl compound and the enolate of an alpha-acidic carbonyl compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000275`** | **Stork-Crabtree hydrogenation** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041) | A hydrogenation in which a metal-ligating group distant from the reacting alkene directs the attack of hydrogen, giving preferentially one stereoisomer of the product. |
| **`RXNO:0000276`** | **Aufbau reaction** | **`is_a`:** polymerisation reaction (RXNO:0000246)<br>**Synonyme:** "Aufbau" reaction, 'Aufbau' reaction, Aufbau process | The insertion of alkenes (usually ethene) into the Al-C bond of an aluminium alkyl, to give a long-chain aluminium alkyl that can go on to react again, or can decompose into the corresponding terminal alkene. The addition of transition metals results in reliable production of high molecular weight polymers. |
| **`RXNO:0000277`** | **hydroalumination** | **`is_a`:** hydrometallation (RXNO:0000294) | The reaction of an aluminium alkyl with an alkene, to give an intermediate aluminium species that can give various products after work-up. |
| **`RXNO:0000278`** | **hydrovinylation** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** 1,2-hydrovinylation | The reaction of two alkenes to give an elongated alkene, employing a transition-metal-based catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000279`** | **1,4-hydrovinylation** | **`is_a`:** hydrovinylation (RXNO:0000278) | The reaction of a 1,3-diene with an alkene to give a 1,4-diene, usually employing a cobalt-based catalyst (see Hilt and Luers, Synthesis, 2002, 609). |
| **`RXNO:0000280`** | **alkene metathesis** | **`is_a`:** multiple-bond metathesis (RXNO:0000204)<br>**Synonyme:** Grubbs metathesis, Grubbs reaction, cross metathesis, cross-metathesis, olefin metathesis, transalkylidenation | A joining reaction in which two carbon-carbon double bonds are redistributed among two fragments, i.e. two alkenes. | **`RXNO:0000281`** | **alkyne metathesis** | **`is_a`:** multiple-bond metathesis (RXNO:0000204) | A joining reaction in which two carbon-carbon triple bonds are redistributed among two fragments, i.e. two alkynes. It is catalysed by a metal carbenoid, and proceeds via two four-membered intermediates (metallacyclobutenes). The reaction is driven by release of a low-MW alkyne. |
| **`RXNO:0000282`** | **enyne metathesis** | **`is_a`:** multiple-bond metathesis (RXNO:0000204) | A joining reaction in which a carbon-carbon double bond and a carbon-carbon triple bond are redistributed among two fragments, i.e. an alkene and an alkyne. It is typically catalysed by a metal carbenoid, and proceeds via two four-membered intermediates (metallacyclobutenes). As a low-MW alkene is not released, the reaction is usually driven by formation of a ring. |
| **`RXNO:0000283`** | **ring-closing enyne metathesis** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** ring closing enyne metathesis | A joining reaction in which a carbon-carbon double bond and a carbon-carbon triple bond in the same molecule are redistributed to give a alkene-substituted cycloalkene. It is typically catalysed by a metal carbenoid, and proceeds via two four-membered intermediates (metallacyclobutanes). The reaction is driven by formation of the ring. |
| **`RXNO:0000284`** | **ring-opening metathesis** | **`is_a`:** ring breaking (RXNO:0000205)<br>**Synonyme:** ring opening metathesis | A ring-breaking reaction in which two carbon-carbon double bonds are redistributed among two fragments, i.e. an alkene and a cycloalkene. It is typically catalysed by a metal carbenoid, and proceeds via two four-membered intermediates (metallacyclobutanes). The reaction is driven by release of ring strain in the cycloalkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000285`** | **Luche reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction where an aldehyde or ketone reacts with an allyl halide and zinc to give a homoallylic alcohol. |
| **`RXNO:0000286`** | **Luche reduction** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037) | The chemoselective reduction of an enone to give an allylic alcohol, using sodium borohydride and an lanthanoid chloride, typically cerium chloride. |
| **`RXNO:0000287`** | **Lindgren oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012) | The oxidation of an aldehyde to a carboxylic acid with chlorite. |
| **`RXNO:0000288`** | **Rosenmund-von Braun reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** Rosenmund-von Braun synthesis | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction where an aryl halide reacts with copper(I) cyanide to give an aryl nitrile. |
| **`RXNO:0000289`** | **Woehler reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011)<br>**Synonyme:** Woehler synthesis, Wohler reaction, Wohler synthesis | The formation of urea from ammonium cyanate. Ammonium cyanate is unstable, and so is formed in situ from either (a) cyanic acid and ammonia, (b) a metal cyanate and an ammonium salt, or (c) a metal cyanate and ammonia. |
| **`RXNO:0000290`** | **hydrosilylation** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041)<br>**Synonyme:** hydrosilation | The addition of a hydrosilane across the double bond of an alkene or alkyne to give a more substituted silane. Addition to an aldehyde or ketone produces a silyl ether. |
| **`RXNO:0000291`** | **hydroamination** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041) | The addition of the N-H bond of an amine across a double bond of an alkene or alkyne, to give a more substituted amine. |
| **`RXNO:0000292`** | **hydroboration** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041) | The addition of a boron hydride across a double bond of an alkene or alkyne. The boron usually adds to the less substituted carbon. |
| **`RXNO:0000293`** | **hydroboration-oxidation** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041) | The formal addition of water across a double bond of an alkene or alkyne by formation of an organoborane and in situ oxidation, typically with basic peroxide. The product is usually the less substituted alcohol. |
| **`RXNO:0000294`** | **hydrometallation** | **`is_a`:** addition reaction step (RXNO:0000041)<br>**Synonyme:** hydrometalation | The reaction of any of a number of metal hydride species with an alkene or alkyne, to give an intermediate organometal species that can give various products after work-up. |
| **`RXNO:0000295`** | **hydrozirconation** | **`is_a`:** hydrometallation (RXNO:0000294) | The reaction of a organozirconium hydride with an alkene or alkyne, to give an intermediate zirconium species that can give various products after work-up. | **`RXNO:0000296`** | **hydrostannylation** | **`is_a`:** hydrometallation (RXNO:0000294) | The reaction of a organotin hydride with an alkene or alkyne, to give an intermediate tin species that can give various products after work-up. |
| **`RXNO:0000297`** | **hydrotelluration** | **`is_a`:** hydrometallation (RXNO:0000294) | The reaction of a organotellurium hydride with an alkyne, to give a (usually (Z)-configured) vinyltellurium species. |
| **`RXNO:0000298`** | **Fetizon oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012) | The oxidation of a primary alcohol to an aldehyde or a secondary alcohol to a ketone using Fetizon's reagent. |
| **`RXNO:0000299`** | **Vorbrueggen condensation** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** Vorbrueggen glycosylation, Vorbruggen condensation, Vorbruggen glycosylation | The substitution of an acylated sugar with a silylated base to give a nucleoside. |
| **`RXNO:0000300`** | **thiol-ene reaction** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** thiol-ene click reaction | The addition of a thiol across the double bond of an alkene to give a thioether. |
| **`RXNO:0000301`** | **Yamamoto polymerisation** | **`is_a`:** polymerisation reaction (RXNO:0000246)<br>**Synonyme:** Yamamoto coupling, Yamamoto polymerization | The nickel-catalysed carbon-carbon coupling of a dihaloaromatic compound or aromatic bistriflate to give a polymer. |
| **`RXNO:0000302`** | **thermal hydrosilylation** | **`is_a`:** hydrosilylation (RXNO:0000290) | The thermally induced addition of a hydrosilane across the double bond of an alkene or alkyne to give a more substituted silane, usually applied to cases where the hydrosilane is part of a silicon surface. |
| **`RXNO:0000303`** | **photohydrosilylation** | **`is_a`:** hydrosilylation (RXNO:0000290)<br>**Synonyme:** photo-hydrosilylation | The photochemically induced addition of a hydrosilane across the double bond of an alkene or alkyne to give a more substituted silane, usually applied to cases where the hydrosilane is part of a silicon surface. | **`RXNO:0000304`** | **Julia-Kocienski olefination** | **`is_a`:** olefination (RXNO:0000479)<br>**Synonyme:** Julia-Kocienski coupling, Julia-Kocienski reaction | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction between a tetrazole sulfone and an aldehyde or ketone to give a (typically trans) alkene. |
| **`RXNO:0000305`** | **seleno-Pummerer rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** Pummerer rearrangement (RXNO:0000220)<br>**Synonyme:** selenoxide Pummerer reaction | The conversion of a selenoxide with an alpha-hydrogen to an alpha-subtituted selenoether. |

| **`RXNO:0000307`** | **Mori-Ban indole synthesis** | **`is_a`:** indole synthesis (RXNO:0000395)<br>**Synonyme:** Mori-Ban indole reaction | An intramolecular Heck reaction of an ortho-halo-aniline with an alkene, catalysed by palladium. |
| **`RXNO:0000308`** | **Yamaguchi lactonisation** | **`is_a`:** Yamaguchi esterification (RXNO:0000309), oxacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000346)<br>**Synonyme:** Yamaguchi lactonization | The intramolecular esterification of a carboxylic acid group with an alcohol group using 2,4,6-trichlorobenzoyl chloride (Yamaguchi reagent) - an intramolecular Yamaguchi esterification. |
| **`RXNO:0000309`** | **Yamaguchi esterification** | **`is_a`:** substitution step (RXNO:0000331) | The esterification of a carboxylic acid with an alcohol using 2,4,6-trichlorobenzoyl chloride (Yamaguchi reagent). |
| **`RXNO:0000310`** | **oxa-Diels-Alder reaction** | **`is_a`:** hetero-Diels-Alder reaction (RXNO:0000313)<br>**Synonyme:** oxa Diels-Alder cycloaddition, oxa Diels-Alder reaction, oxa-Diels-Alder cycloaddition, oxo Diels-Alder cycloaddition, oxo Diels-Alder reaction, oxo-Diels-Alder cycloaddition, oxo-Diels-Alder reaction | A Diels-Alder reaction between a diene and an aldehyde or ketone to give a six-membered oxygen-containing ring compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000311`** | **Tsuji-Trost reaction** | **`is_a`:** allylation (MOP:0000422), carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** Trost allylation | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction in which a nucleophile reacts with an allyl acetate, allyl halide, allyl carbonate, allyl carbamate, allyl phosphate, allyl sulfone or allyl nitro compound to give an allyl compound. | **`RXNO:0000312`** | **Clauson-Kaas reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring rearrangement (RXNO:0000202), pyrrole synthesis (RXNO:0000497)<br>**Synonyme:** Clauson-Kaas pyrrole synthesis | A ring rearrangement reaction where an amine reacts with a 2,5-dialkoxytetrahydrofuran to give a pyrrole. |
| **`RXNO:0000313`** | **hetero-Diels-Alder reaction** | **`is_a`:** Diels-Alder reaction (RXNO:0000006) | A [4+2] cycloaddition reaction where a diene (or heterocyclic analogue) reacts with a dienophile to form an unsaturated heterocyclic six-membered ring compound. |
| **`RXNO:0000316`** | **palladium-catalysed carbon-carbon coupling reaction** | **`is_a`:** carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction that is catalysed by a palladium atom. |
| **`RXNO:0000317`** | **ester condensation reaction** | **`is_a`:** condensation reaction (MOP:0000627) | A condensation reaction that results in the production of an ester. |

| **`RXNO:0000655`** | **Favorskii reaction** | **`is_a`:** planned reaction step (RXNO:0000001), carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002)<br>**Synonyme:** Faworski-Babayan reaction | A reaction between an alkyne and a carbonyl group under basic conditions in which a terminal alkyne serves as the CH-acidic moiety. When the carbonyl is an aldehyde, the 'Favorskii rearrangement' (RXNO:0000385) takes place and leads to an enone. |

In the process, diimide is oxidized to dinitrogen. |

| **`RXNO:0000659`** | **Miyaura borylation** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000), palladium-catalysed carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000316)<br>**Synonyme:** Miyaura boration, Miyaura borylation reaction | A chemical reaction that allows the generation of boronates from vinyl or aryl halides with the cross-coupling of bis(pinacolato)diboron under basic conditions. |
| **`RXNO:0000660`** | **Corey-Winter elimination** | **`is_a`:** molecular skeleton elimination reaction (RXNO:0000123)<br>**Synonyme:** Corey-Winter fragmentation, Corey-Winter olefination, Corey–Winter olefin synthesis | A chemical reaction for the stereoselective synthesis of an olefin from a 1,2-diol. |

| **`RXNO:0000663`** | **nitroxide exchange reaction** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | Nitroxide exchange reactions are based on thermal C–O bond homolysis of alkoxyamines, which leads to transient carbon-centred radicals and persistent nitroxide radicals. Usually these carbon centred radicals are quickly trapped by the nitroxide radicals and reform the alkoxyamines. |
| **`RXNO:0000664`** | **Baeyer pyridine synthesis** | **`is_a`:** functional group modification (RXNO:0000011) | The conversion of pyran or pyrone (pyranone) derivatives to the corresponding pyridine derivative. |

Upon the addition of base, the salt rearranges intramolecularly to aldehydes and ketones, respectively. |
| **`RXNO:0000666`** | **Corey-Suggs oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** PCC oxidation | Oxidation of primary and secondary alcohols to aldehydes and ketones, respectively, through the use of pyridinium chlorochromate (PCC). |
| **`RXNO:0000667`** | **Corey-Schmidt oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** PDC oxidation | Oxidation of primary and secondary alcohols to aldehydes and ketones, respectively, through the use of pyridinium dichromate (PDC). |
| **`RXNO:0000668`** | **Albright-Goldman oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012) | A particularly mild oxidation method in which, for example, dimethyl sulfoxide and acetic anhydride can be used as oxidizing agents to selectively produce aldehydes from primary alcohols without further oxidation to carboxylic acids. Analogously, secondary alcohols can be oxidized to form ketones. |

| **`RXNO:0000671`** | **Baeyer oxindole synthesis** | **`is_a`:** indole synthesis (RXNO:0000395)<br>**Synonyme:** Baeyer indolinone synthesis | The preparation of oxindole/indolin-2-one from 2-nitrophenylacetic acid by reduction of the nitro group and subsequent dehydration. |
| **`RXNO:0000672`** | **Leimgruber-Batcho indole synthesis** | **`is_a`:** indole synthesis (RXNO:0000395)<br>**Synonyme:** Batcho-Leimgruber indole synthesis | Condensation of a formamide acetal with a derivative of o-nitrotoluene to yield an enamine intermediate that subsequently undergoes reductive cyclization to give the corresponding indole. |
| **`RXNO:0000673`** | **IBX oxidation** | **`is_a`:** functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012) | Oxidation of alcohols to the corresponding carbonyl product using 2-iodoxybenzoic acid (IBX) as oxidizing agent. |
| **`RXNO:0000674`** | **decarboxylative cross-coupling** | **`is_a`:** cross-coupling (RXNO:0000603) | A chemical reaction in which a carboxylic acid (or its salt) reacts with an organic (alkyl or aryl) halide to form a new carbon-carbon bond with concomitant loss of CO2. In addition, the reaction requires a metal catalyst, a base and an oxidizing agent. |
| **`RXNO:0000675`** | **Balsohn alkylation** | **`is_a`:** planned reaction step (RXNO:0000001), carbon-carbon coupling reaction (RXNO:0000002) | A carbon-carbon coupling reaction between an arene and an alkene to give a substituted arene. The reaction is catalysed by a Lewis acid. |
| **`RXNO:0000676`** | **Kabachnik-Fields reaction** | **`is_a`:** dehydration reaction (MOP:0000628), molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000)<br>**Synonyme:** phospha-Mannich reaction | A three-component condensation reaction in which primary or secondary amines react with a carbonyl compound (aldehyde or ketone) and a dialkyl phosphonate (also called dialkyl phosphite) to form an alpha-aminophosphonate. |
| **`RXNO:0000677`** | **Baekeland-Manasse-Lederer reaction** | **`is_a`:** polymerisation reaction (RXNO:0000246) | A polymerisation reaction in which phenol and formaldehyde react together in a chain reaction to form a phenol formaldehyde resin (also infrequently called phenoplast). |
| **`RXNO:0000678`** | **Aston-Greenburg rearrangement** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The rearrangement of an alpha-haloketone into an ester with a tertiary alpha-carbon atom by intramolecular migration of an alkyl or aryl group. |
| **`RXNO:0000679`** | **Baddeley isomerization** | **`is_a`:** rearrangement step (RXNO:0000025) | The rearrangement/migration of alkyl groups in polyalkylbenzenes or polynuclear aromatic compounds in the presence of anhydrous aluminum chloride or the mixture of protonic acid and Lewis acid. |
| **`RXNO:0000680`** | **Fukuyama reduction** | **`is_a`:** functional group reduction (RXNO:0000037) | The reduction of a thioester to an aldehyde by a silyl hydride in the presence of catalytic amounts of palladium. |
| **`RXNO:0000681`** | **Dötz reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005)<br>**Synonyme:** Doetz reaction, Wulff-Dötz reaction, benzannulation reaction of Fischer carbene complexes | A three component cyclization of an aromatic or vinylic alkoxy pentacarbonyl chromium carbene complex, an alkyne and carbon monoxide to give a Cr(CO)3 coordinated phenol. |
| **`RXNO:0000682`** | **Kulinkovich reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | The formation of a substituted cyclopropanol by reaction of a carboxylic ester with a Grignard reagent (carrying a hydrogen atom in the beta-position) in the presence of a titanium(IV) alkoxide as catalyst. A modified cyclopropanol product can be generated by addition of a terminal alkene as a further reactant. |
| **`RXNO:0000683`** | **Kulinkovich-de Meijere reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | The formation of a substituted cyclopropylamine by reaction of an amide with a Grignard reagent (carrying a hydrogen atom in the beta-position) in the presence of a titanium(IV) alkoxide as catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000684`** | **Kulinkovich-Szymoniak reaction** | **`is_a`:** ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | The formation of a substituted primary cyclopropylamine by reaction of a nitrile with a Grignard reagent (carrying a hydrogen atom in the beta-position) in the presence of a titanium(IV) alkoxide as catalyst. |
| **`RXNO:0000686`** | **Jacobsen epoxidation** | **`is_a`:** epoxidation (MOP:0000671), oxacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000346)<br>**Synonyme:** Jacobsen-Katsuki epoxidation | Chiral (salen)manganese(III)-catalyzed asymmetric epoxidation of alkenes. In addition to the catalyst, stoichiometric amounts of an oxidant (e.g. sodium hypochlorite) are required. |
| **`RXNO:0000687`** | **Shi epoxidation** | **`is_a`:** epoxidation (MOP:0000671), oxacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000346) | Organocatalytic enantioselective epoxidation of trans- and tri-substituted olefins using a fructose derived chiral ketone catalyst and potassium peroxomonosulfate or H2O2 as an oxidant. |
| **`RXNO:0000688`** | **Asinger reaction** | **`is_a`:** azacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000347), thiacycle synthesis (RXNO:0000348)<br>**Synonyme:** Asinger thiazoline synthesis, Asinger-4 component reaction (A-4CR) | A multicomponent reaction (A-4CR) in which 3-thiazolines (2,5-dihydro-1,3-thiazoles) or mixtures of several 3-thiazolines are synthesized from elemental sulfur, an alpha-substitutable ketone or aldehyde, another carbonyl component and ammonia. |
| **`RXNO:0000689`** | **haloform reaction** | **`is_a`:** halogenation (MOP:0000550), end-of-chain chain shortening (RXNO:0000105)<br>**Synonyme:** Einhorn reaction, Lieben reaction | Cleavage of methyl ketones (i.e. oxidative demethylation) with halogens (or hypohalites) in the presence of a base to give a carboxylic acid and haloform (trihalomethane). |
| **`RXNO:0000690`** | **Atherton-Todd reaction** | **`is_a`:** halogenation (MOP:0000550), functional group oxidation (RXNO:0000012)<br>**Synonyme:** AT reaction | The conversion of dialkyl phosphites (dialkyl phosphonates) to dialkyl chlorophosphates using tetrachloromethane and a base (usually a primary, secondary or tertiary amine). |
| **`RXNO:0000691`** | **Baeyer diarylmethane synthesis** | **`is_a`:** dehydration reaction (MOP:0000628), molecular skeleton joining reaction (RXNO:0000000), ring formation reaction step (RXNO:0000005) | Synthesis of diarylmethane derivatives using an aldehyde and an aromatic compound at low temperature in an acidic medium. |
| **`RXNO:0000692`** | **silyl deprotection** | **`is_a`:** hydroxy group deprotection (RXNO:0000430) | A hydroxyl group deprotection in which an silyl ether group is transformed into a hydroxy group. |
